# Verify proposed fixes to LR explanations

Another worker proposed edits to LSAT U's LR Question Explanations. You are the independent check. Nothing is applied unless you approve it. Be skeptical: your job is to catch edits that are wrong, not to admire them.

## Inputs
- The proposals: a JSON list at the path you are given. Each has qid, table, row_id, column, style, letter (for notes), old, new, kind, why, evidence.
- Read-only mirror of the database: `C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/system/scripts/lsatlab-pull/data/db/` (`lr_questions.json` has stimulus, prompt = stem, correct_choice; `lr_answer_choices.json` has choice_text, tag per letter; `lr_explanations.json`, `lr_explanation_steps.json`, `lr_explanation_choice_notes.json`).
- The bank students read: `C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu/public/data/PT{n}-S{s}-LR.json`.
Load JSON with Python (`python -I`), never whole files into context. Write scripts only in your work directory.

## For every proposal
Read the question's stimulus, stem, all five choices, the key, and the old and new text in full. Then decide:
- **approve** — the new text is correct: every quote matches the choice or stimulus exactly, the logic is right for this question and key, the verdict for the letter agrees with the key (correct letter is argued as correct, wrong letters as wrong), nothing outside the defect was damaged, and it reads clearly.
- **fix** — the edit is the right idea but has a mistake you can correct. Give the full corrected `new`.
- **reject** — the edit is wrong, unnecessary, or makes things worse. Say why.

Hard rules for approve/fix: no added em dashes; no added gendered pronouns (he, she, his, her...) for people in the stimulus; no mention of Logic Games; never change which choice is credited; a wrong-answer note must say why that choice is wrong, a correct-answer note why it is right.

Also settle any open question the proposer raised in its `why` (for example a disputed verdict). If you cannot settle it with confidence, reject that edit and say what is needed.

## Output
Write JSON Lines to the output path you are given, one per proposal, in input order:
`{"row_id": "...", "qid": "...", "verdict": "approve|fix|reject", "new": "<only when fix: full corrected text>", "note": "one sentence"}`

Reply with counts of approve, fix, reject, and list every reject and fix with its one-sentence reason.

"""Build guarded UPDATE batches from edits-X.json + verdicts-X.jsonl.
Usage: python -I apply.py <edits.json> <verdicts.jsonl> <out_prefix>
Writes <out_prefix>-NN.sql (each <=~60KB) and <out_prefix>-final.json (the applied set, old kept as snapshot)."""
import json, sys, re
GENDER = re.compile(r"\b(he|she|him|her|his|hers|himself|herself)\b", re.I)
edits = json.load(open(sys.argv[1], encoding='utf-8'))
verd = [json.loads(l) for l in open(sys.argv[2], encoding='utf-8') if l.strip()]
byrow = {}
for v in verd: byrow[v['row_id']] = v
final, bad = [], []
for p in edits:
    v = byrow.get(p['row_id'])
    if not v: bad.append(('no verdict', p['qid'], p['row_id'])); continue
    if v['verdict'] == 'reject': continue
    new = v['new'] if v['verdict'] == 'fix' else p['new']
    if not new or not new.strip(): bad.append(('empty', p['qid'])); continue
    if new.count('—') > p['old'].count('—'): bad.append(('em dash', p['qid'], p['row_id'])); continue
    if len(GENDER.findall(new)) > len(GENDER.findall(p['old'])): bad.append(('pronoun', p['qid'], p['row_id'])); continue
    if 'logic game' in new.lower() and 'logic game' not in p['old'].lower(): bad.append(('LG', p['qid'])); continue
    final.append(dict(p, new=new, verdict=v['verdict']))
print('final', len(final), 'held back', len(bad))
for b in bad: print('  ', b)
json.dump(final, open(sys.argv[3] + '-final.json', 'w', encoding='utf-8'), ensure_ascii=False, indent=0)
q = lambda s: "'" + s.replace("'", "''") + "'"
groups = {}
for p in final: groups.setdefault((p['table'], p['column']), []).append(p)
n = 0
for (t, c), rows in groups.items():
    chunk, size = [], 0
    def flush():
        global n
        if not chunk: return
        n += 1
        vals = ',\n'.join(f"({q(r['row_id'])}::uuid,{q(r['old'])},{q(r['new'])})" for r in chunk)
        sql = (f"update {t} t set {c}=v.new from (values\n{vals}\n) v(id,old,new) "
               f"where t.id=v.id and t.{c}=v.old returning t.id;")
        open(f"{sys.argv[3]}-{n:02d}.sql", 'w', encoding='utf-8').write(sql)
        print(f"{sys.argv[3]}-{n:02d}.sql", t, c, len(chunk))
    for r in rows:
        s = len(r['old']) + len(r['new'])
        if size + s > 60000: flush(); chunk, size = [], 0
        chunk.append(r); size += s
    flush()
"""python -I batch.py <rows.json> <outdir>: guarded UPDATE batches (<45KB) plus .n row counts."""
import json, os, sys
rows_in = json.load(open(sys.argv[1], encoding='utf-8'))
out = sys.argv[2]; os.makedirs(out, exist_ok=True)
q = lambda s: "'" + s.replace("'", "''") + "'"
g = {}
for p in rows_in: g.setdefault((p['table'], p['column']), []).append(p)
n = 0
for (t, c), rows in g.items():
    i = 0
    while i < len(rows):
        ch, size = [], 0
        while i < len(rows) and (not ch or size + len(rows[i]['old']) + len(rows[i]['new']) < 45000):
            ch.append(rows[i]); size += len(rows[i]['old']) + len(rows[i]['new']); i += 1
        n += 1
        vals = ',\n'.join(f"({q(r['row_id'])}::uuid,{q(r['old'])},{q(r['new'])})" for r in ch)
        open(f'{out}/{n:02d}.sql', 'w', encoding='utf-8').write(
            f"update {t} t set {c}=v.new from (values\n{vals}\n) v(id,old,new) where t.id=v.id and t.{c}=v.old returning t.id;")
        open(f'{out}/{n:02d}.n', 'w').write(str(len(ch)))
print(n, 'batches')

"""Merge cross-slice duplicate edits. Writes merged-final.json (all rows, deduped) and conflicts.json."""
import json, difflib, re
S = 'A B C D1 D2 D3'.split()
rows = {}
for s in S:
    for p in json.load(open(f'sql{s}-final.json', encoding='utf-8')):
        rows.setdefault((p['row_id'], p['column']), []).append(dict(p, slice=s))

def hunks(old, new):
    sm = difflib.SequenceMatcher(None, old, new, autojunk=False)
    return [(i1, i2, new[j1:j2]) for t, i1, i2, j1, j2 in sm.get_opcodes() if t != 'equal']

def merge(old, a, b):
    if a == b: return a
    ha, hb = hunks(old, a), hunks(old, b)
    allh = sorted([(h, 'a') for h in ha] + [(h, 'b') for h in hb], key=lambda x: (x[0][0], x[0][1]))
    out, pos, last_end = [], 0, -1
    for (i1, i2, txt), who in allh:
        if i1 < pos or (i1 == pos and i1 == i2 and last_end == i1 and out):  # overlap / same insertion point
            if i1 < pos: return None
        out.append(old[pos:i1]); out.append(txt); pos = i2; last_end = i2
    out.append(old[pos:])
    return ''.join(out)

final, conflicts = [], []
for k, ps in rows.items():
    if len(ps) == 1: final.append(ps[0]); continue
    a, b = ps[0], ps[1]
    if a['old'] != b['old']: conflicts.append({'k': k, 'why': 'old differs', 'ps': ps}); continue
    m = merge(a['old'], a['new'], b['new'])
    if m is None: conflicts.append({'k': k, 'why': 'overlap', 'ps': ps}); continue
    final.append(dict(a, new=m, slice=a['slice'] + '+' + b['slice']))
json.dump(final, open('merged-final.json', 'w', encoding='utf-8'), ensure_ascii=False)
json.dump(conflicts, open('conflicts.json', 'w', encoding='utf-8'), ensure_ascii=False, indent=1)
print('rows', len(final), 'merged', sum('+' in p['slice'] for p in final), 'conflicts', len(conflicts))

## assistant

Using merged hunks, the expression appears once per row. Each batch is a single statement, so if any patched row fails its md5 check, a forced divide-by-zero rolls back the entire batch.

35:const q = await fetch('/my/services/get-questions-just-props.php').then(r => r.text());
# Read-only REST pull of the LR question + explanation tables. Run from the vault root.
# Usage: python system/scripts/lsatlab-pull/db_pull.py [out_dir] [table ...]  (default: data/db next to this file; the four LR explanation tables)
import sys
from pathlib import Path
sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]))
from project_paths import project_path, VAULT_ROOT
import sys
import json, re, urllib.request, os
env={}
for f in [VAULT_ROOT / '.env', project_path('lsatu', '.env')]:
  for l in open(f,encoding='utf-8'):
    m=re.match(r'([A-Z_]+)=(.*)',l.strip())
    if m: env[m[1]]=m[2].strip('"')
U=env['VITE_SUPABASE_URL'];K=env['VITE_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY']
def req(p,body=None,tok=None):
  r=urllib.request.Request(U+p,data=json.dumps(body).encode() if body else None,headers={'apikey':[REDACTED],'Content-Type':'application/json','Authorization':[REDACTED] or K)})
  return json.load(urllib.request.urlopen(r))
tok=req('/auth/v1/token?grant_type=password',{'email':env['LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL'],'password':[REDACTED]})['access_token']
out=sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv)>1 else os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)),'data','db')
os.makedirs(out,exist_ok=True)
for t in (sys.argv[2:] or ['lr_questions','lr_explanations','lr_explanation_steps','lr_explanation_choice_notes']):
  rows=[];o=0
  while True:
    b=req(f'/rest/v1/{t}?select=*&limit=1000&offset={o}',tok=tok);rows+=b;o+=1000
    if len(b)<1000:break
  json.dump(rows,open(os.path.join(out,t+'.json'),'w',encoding='utf-8'),ensure_ascii=False)
  print(t,len(rows), sorted(rows[0].keys()) if rows else '')

## assistant

Step 2 is finished: all the verified explanation fixes are now in the live LSAT U database, and I checked the result.

**The handoff was wrong.** It said nothing had been written for step 2 and that slice C still needed its verifier run. In fact a later session (03:22 to 03:42 on 2026-10-08) had already verified every slice, A through D, and merged them into one set of 849 edits. It wrote about a quarter of them and stopped without logging anything. Both timed catalogs were also already switched to the new hashes. So there was no slice C verifier to rerun.

**What I did:**
- I wrote the 639 rows that were still on the old text, in 17 batches. Each write applied only if the row still held the expected old text and the result came out exactly as planned; any mismatch would have cancelled the whole batch. None did.
- I pulled a fresh copy of the database and compared every row's full text against the plan. 846 of 849 match exactly.
- The other 3 already hold a hand-edited version that reads better than the plan, so I left them alone. One of them (`c2b6fc9c`) also removes a "his".

**One thing I didn't check myself:** the earlier session ran the editorial review, which approved, fixed or rejected each edit for LSAT accuracy. I'm relying on those verdicts. My checks confirm only that the database now holds exactly the approved text.

I logged this in `corpus/lr-explanations/fixes/index.md` and `wiki/log.md`. Nothing was committed or pushed.

**Next is step 3:** rewriting the 192 "Revamp" explanations, one question type at a time. I haven't started it. The open slice C questions (PT157-S3-Q25 D, PT151-S2-Q18 B, PT124-S3-Q3) were settled by the verifier as part of the applied set, so they're no longer open.
